Proposed final program:
```
import numpy as np
try: from scipy.signal import fftconvolve
except: use np.convolve for small? scipy available.

def _score_np(a):
    # avoid repeated list conversion? evaluate_sequence expects list; uses np.convolve. We can compute quickly with numpy: a clipped.
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=float); a=np.clip(a,0,1000); if len=0 or sum<0.01 return inf; n=len; b=np.convolve(a,a); return float(2*n*b.max()/sum(a)**2)
```

Local optimization details:
Objective F(a)=max c_k. Scale invariant. We can fix sum = n (midpoint cosine sum n). Need minimize max c subject to a>=0, sum=n. This is nonconvex but subgradient works.

Compute c = fftconvolve(a,a, mode='full') length 2n-1.
Find k*=argmax(c).
Compute gradient of c_k w.r.t a:
For each j, grad[j] = 2*a[k-j] if 0 <= k-j < n and 2j != k; = a[k-j] if 2j == k; else 0.
Vectorized:
```
idx = k - np.arange(n)
valid = (idx >=0)&(idx<n)
grad = np.zeros(n)
grad[valid] = 2*a[idx[valid]]
diagonal = (2*np.arange(n) == k) & valid
grad[diagonal] = a[idx[diagonal]]
```
Then update a_new = a - step*grad. To keep sum fixed, subtract mean grad or use projection onto sum constraint. Since clip changes sum, renormalize.
But if we subtract mean grad, the sum unchanged before clip. Use `grad -= grad.mean()`.
Step size: Need scale. c ~ n/2 ~2403, gradient ~2*a ~O(1). If step=0.5, change O(0.5), relative a O(1). Good. Could use adaptive `step = max(1e-6, min(2.0, 0.1 * n / c_max))`? c_max ~n/2, n/c_max ~2 => step ~0.2. Use decreasing.
But nonconvex may increase max temporarily; we can use only accept if surrogate score improves? Could do line search: try step = base, base/2, etc, evaluate surrogate max via FFT, accept best. This costs several FFTs per iter. n=4806, FFT size ~16384, fast. We can do maybe thousands iterations in seconds.

However, gradient at single k* might bias to reduce the maximum lag only, possibly increasing others. Softmax gradient uses all lags and may be better.
Compute weights w = exp(tau*(c - c.max())) normalized. tau = (2n-1)/sum? Use softmax. Gradient = 2*correlate(w, a, mode='valid')? Need check orientation.
We want ∂/∂a_j sum_k w_k c_k. c_k=Σ_i a_i a_{k-i}. Derivative ignoring diagonal = 2 Σ_i w_{i+j} a_i for i such that i+j=k? Let's derive with orientation:
For fixed a_j, terms in c_k involving a_j: a_j*a_{k-j} (i=j) and a_{j}*? Actually c_k=Σ_i a_i a_{k-i}. ∂c_k/∂a_j = a_{k-j} if 0<=k-j<n plus a_{k-j} if 0<=k-j<n (same) except diagonal. So approx 2 a_{k-j}. Sum_k w_k 2 a_{k-j} = 2 Σ_{k: i?} w_k a_{k-j}. Let i=k-j => k=i+j, i from 0..n-1. term 2 a_i w_{i+j}. So grad_j ≈ 2 Σ_i w_{i+j} a_i. This is cross-correlation of w (length 2n-1) and a (length n) where result[j]=Σ_i w[i+j] a[i]. `scipy.signal.correlate(w, a, mode='valid')`? Let's verify SciPy correlate: correlate(a, v) = sum_n a[n+k] conj(v[n])? Actually `signal.correlate(c訊, signal, mode)`: result[n] = sum_m c訊[m+n] * signal[m] for mode valid? From docs: y[n] = sum_m x[m+n] * conj(v[m]). If x=w, v=a, mode valid length len(w)-len(a)+1 = n, result[j]= sum_m w[m+j] conj(a[m]). Yes exactly. So grad=2*correlate(w,a,'valid').
Diagonal corrections: For each j, if 2j=k? For smooth sum, exact gradient = 2*sum_i w_{i+j}a_i - correction for i=j? Let's derive: derivative exact includes 2 a_{k-j} except diagonal where k=2j (i=j) gives a_j. For each k, when i=k-j=j (k=2j), we doubled incorrectly by 1*a_j. So subtract Σ_k w_k a_j * 1_{k=2j} = w_{2j} a_j. Thus exact grad = 2*correlate(w,a,'valid') - w[2j]*a_j. w[2j] exists for j=0..n-1 (2j up to 2n-2). Good.

```
def _fft_convolve(a):
    # use scipy.signal.fftconvolve if available, fallback np.convolve
    try: from scipy.signal import fftconvolve; return fftconvolve(a,a,mode='full')
    except: return np.convolve(a,a)
```

def cosine_mid(n, scale=1.0):
    if n == 1: return np.array([1.0])
    i=np.arange(n)
    a=1 - np.cos(np.pi*(i+0.5)/n)
    a = a * (n / a.sum()) # sum n
    return a * scale?
```

try:
    from scipy.signal import fftconvolve, correlate
    _HAVE_SCIPY_SIGNAL = True
except Exception:
    _HAVE_SCIPY_SIGNAL = False
```

Let's draft final code:

```
import numpy as np
import time

def _as_valid_np(seq):
    try:
       a = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
       if a.ndim != 1 or a.size == 0: return None
       a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
       if not np.isfinite(a).all(): return None
       if a.sum() < 0.01: return None
       return a
    except Exception:
       return None

def _score_list(a):
    # exact
    return float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in a]))

def _cosine_mid(n):
    n=int(n)
    if n <=0: return None
    if n == 1: return np.ones(1)
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a = 1.0 - np.cos(np.pi * (i + 0.5) / n)
    # sum should be n; normalize to sum=n
    s=a.sum()
    if s <=0: a=np.ones(n)
    else: a = a * (n/s)
    return a

def _cosine_endpoint(n): ... optional

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    start = time.monotonic()
    budget = max(1.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = start + budget - 10.0
    if deadline < start + 1: deadline = start + budget - 1
    n_parent = 0
    parent = None
    try:
        p = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
        if p.ndim == 1 and p.size>0 and np.isfinite(p).all():
            p = np.clip(p,0,1000)
            if p.sum() >=0.01:
                parent = p
                n_parent = p.size
    except Exception:
        parent = None
    n = n_parent if n_parent else 4806
    candidates = []
    if parent is not None: candidates.append(parent)
    c1 = _cosine_mid(n)
    if c1 is not None: candidates.append(c1)
    if n > 1:
       c2 = 1.0 - np.cos(np.pi*np.arange(n)/(n-1)); c2*= n/c2.sum(); candidates.append(c2)
    # maybe a uniform? parent baseline enough.
    best = None
    best_score = np.inf
    for cand in candidates:
       a = _as_valid_np(cand)
       if a is None: continue
       # scale to max 1 for final? But exact score invariant. Keep working sum maybe.
       # For baseline exact, use a as is. But if parent huge, direct conv may be big but clipped 1000. okay.
       try:
          sc = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in a]))
       except Exception:
          sc = np.inf
       if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
          best_score = sc
          best = a.copy()
    # If no best, use cosine mid scaled.
    if best is None:
       if c1 is not None: best=c1
       else: best=np.ones(4806)
       best_score = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in best]))

    # Optional local polish on best if n between 10 and 20000 and time
    # Work with a copy, normalize sum to n for stable step.
    if n >= 10 and n <= 20000 and best_score > 1.05: # only if needed? But best_score maybe ~1, skip. Could always run a little.
        try:
            a = best.copy().astype(np.float64)
            s=a.sum()
            if s <=0: a=np.ones(n); s=n
            a = a * (n/s)
            a = np.clip(a, 0, 1000)
            # if scipy available
            if _HAVE_SCIPY_SIGNAL:
                step = 0.2
                local_best_score = best_score
                local_best = best.copy()
                local_start = time.monotonic()
                max_local_time = min(120.0, max(1.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 5.0))
                for it in range(5000):
                    if time.monotonic() > deadline - 6.0: break
                    if time.monotonic() - local_start > max_local_time: break
                    c = fftconvolve(a,a,mode='full')
                    if not np.isfinite(c).all(): break
                    # occasionally exact
                    if it % 20 == 0:
                         sc = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in a]))
                         if sc < local_best_score:
                            local_best_score = sc; local_best=a.copy()
                    # compute smooth grad
                    # tau based on top difference: find top few? use 10.
                    tau = 10.0
                    expw = np.exp(tau*(c - c.max()))
                    w = expw / expw.sum()
                    grad = 2.0 * correlate(w, a, mode='valid')
                    if not np.isfinite(grad).all(): break
                    grad -= grad.mean()
                    # step update with line search among factors
                    accepted=False
                    base_score = c.max()
                    # current maybe base_score; use surrogate max
                    for fac in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.1):
                         at = a - step*fac*grad
                         at = np.clip(at, 0.0, 1000.0)
                         st = at.sum()
                         if st <= 0: continue
                         at = at * (n/st)
                         at = np.clip(at,0,1000)
                         ct = fftconvolve(at,at,mode='full')
                         mt = ct.max()
                         if mt < base_score - 1e-12:
                             a = at
                             accepted=True
                             break
                         step = step * 0.9
                    if not accepted:
                         step *= 0.85
                    # update step adapt maybe
                    step = min(1.0, max(1e-5, step * 1.0))
                # final exact on local best
                sc = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in local_best]))
                if sc < best_score:
                    best = local_best; best_score = sc
        except Exception:
            pass

    # final: scale best to max 1 (optional) and validate exact
    try:
       aout = np.asarray(best, dtype=np.float64).copy()
       aout = np.clip(aout,0,1000)
       if aout.size ==0: aout=np.array([1.0])
       m = aout.max()
       if m > 0:
           aout = aout / m
           aout = np.clip(aout,0,1.0)
       # exact score after scaling (should same)
       final_score = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in aout]))
       if not np.isfinite(final_score):
           # fallback parent or ones
           aout = np.ones(1)
           final_score = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in aout]))
       return [float(x) for x in aout]
    except Exception:
       return [1.0]
```

The parent SOT describes LP method: Given f with peak M, find g maximizing sum(g) subject to f*g <= M and g>=0, then update. This is like solving dual to reduce peak. It may find sequences with AC1 near target. We need reproduce effective search.

Given time budget and single CPU, for n=4806 we need efficient algorithm. But now we know uniform 2, need reach 1.502; significant decrease. The LP method might work. Let's understand it.

Objective score = 2n max((a*a)_k)/S². Fix S = sqrt(2n) or 1. The SOT: Starting f normalized sum sqrt(2n), M=||f*f||∞. Compute g0 maximizing sum g subject to g>=0 and ||f*g||∞ <= M. Rescale g to normalization, update f←(1-t)f+t g. Repeat. This is a multiplicative/weighed update. Why does it work? It finds g that is "almost feasible" w.r.t current autocorrelation bound M. Since f*g is cross-correlation between current f and new g. If we keep peak <=M, increasing sum reduces M when normalized.

Can implement for n=4806? LP has n=4806 variables and 2n-1=9611 constraints: f*g_k <= M. This is dense (each constraint has n coefficients f). LP with 5k variables, 10k constraints might be heavy but CVXPY with PDLP maybe okay for some iterations? Dense data 5k*10k=48M, memory 384MB maybe high. But we can solve dual? Dual: minimize M * sum y subject to (f * y)_j >= 1, y>=0. Here y length 2n-1 variables (~9611), constraints n (~4806). Dense 48M too. Maybe use sparse? The convolution matrix is Hankel, each row/col has n nonzeros, but overall 48M nonzeros (dense-ish). For n=4806, 48M float64=384MB, maybe near memory limit. Could be too high. Need alternative.

Fourier square-root approach details:
We want p length n (nonnegative) real. Let N=2n-1. q_k=1/N for k=0..N-1. We want linear convolution p*p equal q. Since p length n, convolution length N. Use FFT length N (or power of two >=N) with circular convolution. If we set P = sqrt(Q) with proper phase in Fourier domain of length N, then p = IDFT(P) should satisfy p*p=circular q? But q length N, if P length N, p length N (not n). We need p zero for indices n..N-1. Taking P=sqrt(Q) may produce p support all N. We can choose phase to make p sparser? This is square-root filtering; the ideal linear square root of a uniform square pulse has length N, not n? Wait convolution of length n with itself yields length 2n-1. In Fourier (Z-transform), P(z)^2 = q(z) (polynomial degree 2n-2). Then P(z) is a square root polynomial of degree at most n-1 if choose one of two. So in discrete finite Z-transform, there exists P(z) degree n-1 over complex. Its coefficients are p. In DFT length N=2n-1, z=e^{2πi/N}, P_m are N values, but correspond to a polynomial of degree N-1; requiring p_n...p_{N-1}=0 imposes linear constraints, not automatic from sqrt(Q). Q_m is DFT of q, which is a polynomial degree N-2? q uniform length N. The square root polynomial P of degree n-1 may not match sqrt(Q_m) exactly because polynomial square root may have cancellations; however evaluating on roots, perhaps P_m = ± sqrt(Q_m) with a consistent phase chosen as the minimal phase factor. The linear square root of a signal in Fourier domain (Wiener filter) chooses magnitude sqrt and phase half of log spectral factor, not just half phase of Q (which may be ambiguous). For causal nonnegative? There is spectral factorization: Q_m=|P_m|² for some real P if P_m are DFT of p (real). Since q has nonnegative Fourier? Q_m can be negative for some m (sinc), so real p has P_m conjugate symmetric but P_m²=Q_m; Q_m's sign determines phase difference 0 or π. Need choose P_m with conjugate symmetry such that P_m²=Q_m. This is equivalent to a square root of the Fourier values consistent with a polynomial degree. There may exist because q is all-positive autocorrelation? q is actually the desired autocorrelation of p? Is uniform q positive definite? It must be the autocorrelation of some p if exists. The Fourier transform Q_m = |P_m|² must be nonnegative for all m for a real square root (since |P_m|²>=0). But DFT of uniform q has sign changes (sinc)! Therefore no real p with p*p = uniform q. Indeed, uniform sequence on finite cyclic group has negative Fourier coefficients, not positive semidefinite, so it is not an autocorrelation. Thus exact flat impossible. We want approximate positive q. The LP method adjusts.

Still, a Fourier-based initialization using phase correction (spectral factor) could be good: Given q (designed positive), choose P as causal square root (minimum phase) to get p. But q not positive definite. We can choose a target q that is positive definite, e.g., q = r*r for some desired r, but we want flat.

Alternative: Use optimality conditions from LP. The optimal p and the active lags satisfy something. For maximally flat, the dual weights y_k on high lags are positive only at active constraints, and gradient condition: average of y-weighted correlations equals constant. This resembles solving p such that a weighted autocorrelation is constant. Could use iterative methods:
- Start p.
- Compute d=p*p.
- Identify high lags, set weights w (softmax high temperature).
- Update p to reduce weighted autocorrelation: p_new ∝ p / (w*p?)? For nonnegative quadratic constraints, maybe use multiplicative updates.

The SOT LP update: given f, solve g maximizing sum g with f*g <= M. This is like finding g proportional to 1/(f*y?) at optimum. The dual y supports active constraints. There might be an approximate update: g_j ∝ 1/(f*y)_j? Let's derive LP dual.

Primal: max 1^T g s.t. (F g)_k <= M, g>=0, where (F g)_k = Σ_i f_i g_{k-i}. F is convolution matrix.
Dual: min M 1^T y s.t. (F^T y)_j >=1, y>=0. F^T is same convolution/reversal.
Complementary slackness: g_j>0 => (F^T y)_j=1; y_k>0 => (F g)_k=M.
At optimum, for g_j>0, (F^T y)_j=1. If y concentrated on active high lags, g is the solution to F^T y=1 on support. Hard.

But we can approximate y by softmax of current d. Then compute g = solve/min solve? If we ignore inequalities and just set (F^T y)_j=1 -> g combined with f/y =1. In frequency: F^T is convolution with f_y? (f*y)_j. We need find g such that f * g ≈ 1/y? Not simple.

Native fftconvolve:
```
def _convolve_full(a,b):
   n=len(a)+len(b)-1
   N=1 << (n-1).bit_length()
   fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N); fb=np.fft.rfft(b,N)
   return np.fft.irfft(fa*fb,N)[:n]
```
Since a=b, can square. For correlate:
```
def _correl_valid(w,a):
   N=1 << (len(w)+len(a)-2).bit_length()
   W=np.fft.rfft(w,N); A=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
   r=np.fft.irfft(np.conj(W)*A,N)
   return r[:len(a)]
```
Need ensure no circular negative lags? Yes.

Numerical: r may have tiny imaginary; irfft real.

Local optimizer pseudo:
```
def _polish(a, initial_score, deadline, max_time):
    a = a.astype(float).copy()
    n=len(a); target_sum=n
    s=a.sum(); if s<=0: a=np.ones(n); s=n
    a *= target_sum/s
    a = np.clip(a,0,1000)
    best_a=a.copy(); best_exact=initial_score
    step=0.1
    # surrogate current
    c=_convolve_full(a,a); m=c.max()
    for it in range(MAX):
       if time>deadline-10: break
       # exact periodically
       if it % 50 ==0:
          sc=exact_score(a)
          if sc<best_exact: best_exact=sc; best_a=a.copy()
       # weights
       cmax=c.max()
       if not finite: break
       # tau based on top difference
       # compute maybe 100th percentile
       q = np.percentile(c, 99.5)  # can be costly; simpler cmax - np.mean(c)
       spread = max(1.0, cmax - np.mean(c)) # for uniform spread ~n/2 -> tau small; but we want top?
```

Let's design a heuristic search:
- Start with best initial (likely power law beta=0.5).
- While time:
   - Generate candidate by multiplying a random contiguous block (or multiple blocks) by exp(N(0, sigma)), with sigma adaptive (e.g., 0.05 to 0.3).
   - Optionally mirror with some probability.
   - Evaluate surrogate via FFT (fast) or exact if surrogate lower by threshold.
   - Accept if exact/surrogate improves best.
This is like continuation. The search space high-dimensional, random perturbations may be slow. Gradient faster.

Let's implement log-space optimization! It may be better:
- State v = log(a) (a positive). Initialize a positive (add eps for zeros; our power law no zeros).
- Compute a = n * exp(v - logsumexp(v)) for stability.
- Compute c=conv(a,a). dF/da gradient for F=Σw c: g=2 correlate(w,a).
- v_grad = a * (g - np.mean(a*g))  # since sum a=n, constraint force? Let's derive: log a_i = v_i + log n - log Z. da_i/dv_j = a_i(δ_ij - a_j/n). F_v_j = Σ_i g_i a_i (δ_ij - a_j/n) = a_j (g - (a·g)/n). Yes mean(a*g)=a·g/n.
- Update v <- v - step * v_grad. Since F_v already centered under a? Sum a F_v? Σ a_j F_v_j = Σ a_j g_j - (a·g)/n Σ a_j =0; preserves logZ? It keeps sum automatically in exact arithmetic.

Let's implement log-space polish:
```
def _polish_log(a, deadline, max_time=300):
    n=len(a); a = np.maximum(a, 1e-12); a = a * n/a.sum()
    v = np.log(a) - np.log(a).max()
    # current surrogate
    a = n * exp(v - logsumexp(v))
    c = conv(a); m=c.max(); best_m=m; best_v=v.copy(); best_exact initial? we will track exact.
    step=0.1
    tau=5.0
    for it in ...:
       # if time break
       # weights
       cmax=c.max(); w=softmax(c,tau)
       g=2*correl(w,a)
       vg=a*(g - np.mean(a*g))
       # normalize vg maybe
       # line search in v
       accepted=False
       for fac in [1,0.5,0.25,0.125]:
           vt = v - step*fac*vg
           vt -= vt.max()
           at = n*exp(vt - logsumexp(vt))
           # if at max >1000, penalize skip
          if at.max()>1000: continue
           ct=conv(at); mt=ct.max()
           if mt < m*(1-1e-7): accept
       if not accepted: step*=0.8
       else: step=min(0.5, step*1.05)
       if it%50==0: exact...
       # adapt tau: maybe increase if plateau
       tau = min(20, tau*1.01)
```
But `m` should be current surrogate max (not best) for line search. We compute c at start of loop. After accept update c.

For power law plateau, lower tau may average plateau; higher tau focuses exact max. Use `tau = 5.0 + 5.0*it/1000` capped 20.

Let's think about continuous optimal via variational methods. We want minimize sup g subject to f≥0, ∫f=1. At optimum, g(t) likely equals some upper envelope with active constraints where a dual measure μ (positive on active t) satisfies ∫ μ_k? KKT: For active t where g(t)=M, there are positive weights y(t) and for a_i, derivative of ∫ y g wrt f is constant on support. In continuous: g(t)=∫ f(s)f(t-s)ds. The derivative wrt f(r) of ∫ y(t) g(t) dt is 2∫ y(t) f(t-r) dt (since f^2). KKT says for r in support, ∫ y(t) f(t-r) dt = λ (constant), and y(t)g(t)=y(t)M? y supported where g(t)=M. Thus the optimal f has the property that a certain weighted correlation with y is constant: f ∗ y = λ (on support, maybe with support all [0,1]). This resembles the LP SOT. If y is discrete measures at active lags. We can solve a fixed point: choose y at points where g=M. For continuous, maybe y is a finite set of point masses. Then f * y = constant. This is a convolution equation. The solution f may be a sum of exponentials. For target M=0.751, maybe y has 2 or 3 points. Could solve analytically? Interesting.

If y has point masses at t=a,b,..., then f(r) satisfies Σ c_j f(t_j - r)=λ for r∈[0,1] where defined. This is a functional equation. Could yield piecewise exponentials. But deriving here too much.

The iterative algorithm of SOT approximates this: update f using g from LP, which is related to y. Our gradient with softmax y approximates same.

Maybe to improve, use a "dual ascent" explicitly:
- Maintain weights y over lags (softmax). Define h(r)=∫ y(t) f(t-r) dt (correlate). We want h constant where f>0. Update f ∝ 1/h? Because if h too high, reduce f. This is multiplicative: f_new = f / h (normalized). Let's test: If f*f has high lags, y peaked there, h_i = Σ y_k f_{k-i}. If h_i large, f_i reduced. This is like mirror descent with entropy (our log gradient) with update a ← a / (1+η(h-mean)). Our log gradient uses g=2h, v update `a*(h - mean(a*h))`, exponential not division. For small step similar.

The LP update f←(1-t)f+t g where g maximizes sum with f*g<=M might be approximated by g_i ∝ 1/h_i (from dual complementary). We can implement: compute h=correlate(w,a); set candidate g = 1/(h + eps); normalize sum; then mix a = (1-α)a + α g (or in log). This may be more directly SOT. Let's consider adding as an alternate update in the search.

Algorithm iteration SOT-like:
1. c=a*a, M=c.max().
2. weights y concentrated on lags with c≈M.
3. h = correlate(y, a). (In dual, constraints f*y_j ≥1; optimum g roughly 1/h.)
4. Construct g: ensure nonnegative, sum n. Maybe g_j = a_j / h_j (multiplicative) or 1/h_j. Which is better? The LP variable g is independent of a except constraints f*g≤M. Complementary: (f*y)_j=1 for g_j>0, i.e., h_j=1. The g that satisfies h=1 on support minimizes? But g maybe 1/h. The SOT solved LP directly; it uses g not proportional to a. However, mixing with a multiplicative 1/h is a common iterative algorithm for finding a vector with bounded cross-correlation (power iteration). Let's try both in heuristic.

Update options:
- Mirror gradient: a_new = normalize( a * exp(-step*(h - mean(a*h))) )
- Reciprocal: g=normalize(1/(h+eps)); a_new = (1-α)a + α g.
- Maybe combine: g=normalize( a / h ).

We can implement a search loop that applies one of these updates with line search to reduce surrogate max. This may be more effective than plain gradient.

Let's design a generic `improve` function with several move types:
Given a, weights w (softmax of c), compute h=correlate(w,a).
- Move A: `a - step*(2h - mean(2h))` then normalize.
- Move B: log update: `a * exp(-step*(h - np.mean(a*h)/?))` normalize.
- Move C: `a / (1 + step*(h - mean(a*h)))` normalize (multiplicative ratio).
Evaluate surrogate max for each with line search factors, accept best.
This gives diverse descent directions.

def _mod_power(n, beta, amp, freq, phase):

   a=base*(1+amp*np.sin(2π*freq*i/n+phase))

Polish function details:
```
def _polish_log(a0, exact0, deadline, max_time):
    n=len(a0)
    a=a0.copy()
    # ensure positive floor for log
    a=np.maximum(a,1e-12)
    a*=n/a.sum()
    if a.max()>1000: scale? a=a/ a.max()*? wait sum fixed; if max>1000, split? We'll clip: a=np.clip(a,0,1000); a*=n/a.sum()
    v=np.log(a)-np.log(a).max()
    best_v=v.copy(); best_exact=exact0
    # If exact0 not finite, compute
    step=0.1
    tau=5.0
    last_exact=0
    local_best_surrogate=np.inf
    for it in range(100000):
       if time.monotonic()>deadline: break
       # compute a from v
       a=_from_v(v,n)
       c=_conv_full(a)
       m=c.max()
       # update best surrogate? exact later
       # weights
       cmax=m
       # tau schedule maybe start 3, increase
       tau = min(20.0, 3.0 + it*0.02)
       shift=c-cmax
       # To avoid under/overflow, if tau*max(shift) < -700, okay
       ew=np.exp(tau*shift)
       w=ew/ew.sum()

def _perturb(a, rng, sigma=0.2):
    v=np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-12)); v-=v.max()
    for _ in range(rng.integers(1,4)):
       width=int(rng.integers(max(1,n//50), max(2,n//5)))
       start=rng.integers(0,n-width)
       v[start:start+width]+=rng.normal(0,sigma)
    # global trend
    v += sigma*0.1*rng.normal(0,1,size=n)
    v-=v.max()
    return n*exp(v-logsumexp(v))
```

def _from_v(v,n):

Let's sketch final code fully:

```
import numpy as np
import time

_NFFT = {}
def _fft_len(n):
    # n is sequence length; needed FFT size for conv and correlate
    key = n
    if key in _NFFT: return _NFFT[key]
    L = max(1, 2*n - 1)
    N = 1
    while N < L: N <<=1
    _NFFT[key]=N
    return N

def _conv_full(a):
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    N=_fft_len(n)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A,N)
    return c[:2*n-1]

def _correl_valid(w,a):
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    N=_fft_len(n)
    W=np.fft.rfft(w,N)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    r=np.fft.irfft(np.conj(W)*A,N)
    return r[:n]
```

Check correlation orientation with our formula and FFT. Need be certain. Let's test mentally n=2. w=[w0,w1,w2], a=[a0,a1]. Desired res0=w0a0+w1a1; res1=w1a0+w2a1.
Using r=ifft(conj(W)*A). Circular correlation r[k]=Σ_{m=0}^{N-1} w_{(m+k) mod N} a_m. For k=0: w0a0+w1a1. For k=1: w1a0+w2a1? m=0 w1 a0, m=1 w2 a1. Yes. Good. Conjugate? For real, correlation usually ifft(conj(fft(w))*fft(a)) gives y[k]=Σ w[m+k]a[m]. Yes. Good.

We can use `np.conj(W) * A` and irfft.

`_softmax_weights(c, tau)`:
```
shift=c-c.max()
# If tau*shift.max() =0; exp. To avoid all underflow if tau inf. 
ew = np.exp(tau*shift)
s=ew.sum()
if s==0 or not finite: w=np.zeros_like(c); w[c.argmax()]=1
else w=ew/s
```

Pseudo code:
```
def _polish(a0, best_score, deadline, max_time=None, seed=1):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    n=len(a0)
    a=np.maximum(a0,1e-12).astype(float)
    if a.sum()<1e-12: a=np.ones(n)
    a*=n/a.sum(); a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
    v=np.log(a)-np.max(np.log(a))
    best_local_score=best_score
    best_local_a=a.copy()
    step_rms=0.1
    tau=5.0
    local_start=time.monotonic()
    it=0
    # compute initial surrogate
    a=_from_v(v,n)
    c=_conv_full(a); m=c.max()
    # track last exact
    last_exact_time=0
    for it in range(1000000):
       if time.monotonic()>deadline or (max_time and time.monotonic()-local_start>max_time): break
       if m < best_local_surrogate? not need
       # exact periodically
       if it % 25 ==0 or time.monotonic()-last_exact_time > 1.0:
           sc=_exact_score(a)
           if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_local_score:
               best_local_score=sc; best_local_a=a.copy()
           last_exact_time=time.monotonic()
       # if m improved and large, exact? above
       # weights
       tau = min(25.0, 4.0 + 0.02*it)
       w=_softmax_weights(c,tau)
       h=_correl_valid(w,a)
       g=2*h
       # F_u
       ag=np.dot(a,g) / n
       F=a*(g-ag)
       norm=np.linalg.norm(F)
       if not finite or norm<1e-12:
           # random perturbation restart
           v = _perturb_v(v,rng,0.2); continue
       # try rms values; include current step
       rms_values = sorted({step_rms, step_rms*0.5, step_rms*2.0}, reverse=True?) Better try small to large?
       accepted=False
       best_m=m; best_vt=None; best_at=None; best_ct=None
       for rms in (step_rms, step_rms*0.5, step_rms*0.25, step_rms*2.0):
           vt = v - (rms/(norm*0?))*F ?
```
If F norm maybe ~n? We define `F` = a*(g-ag). Its RMS over n = norm/√n. We want RMS log change ~rms. Update `v - (rms/ (norm/√n))*F` would give norm of Δv = rms*√n? Wait if Δv=scale*F, RMS = scale*norm/√n. We want RMS=rms, scale=rms*√n/norm. So `vt = v - (rms*np.sqrt(n)/norm)*F`. That yields RMS of v change = rms. For n=4806, √n=69. If F norm large, okay. Let's set `scale = rms * np.sqrt(n) / norm`. If F norm zero break.
Then line search rms = [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4]. Start step_rms=0.1. This means v changes by a few percents. Good.
```

           vt -= vt.max()
           at=_from_v(vt,n)
           if at.max()>1000: continue

           # for score, compare mt

               best_m=mt; best_vt=vt; best_at=at; best_ct=ct

Perturb v function:
```
def _perturb_v(v,rng,sigma):
   v=v.copy(); n=len(v)
   for _ in range(int(rng.integers(1,4))):
      width=max(1,int(rng.integers(max(1,n//100), max(2,n//10))))
      start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width+1))
      v[start:start+width]+=rng.normal(0,sigma)
   v-=v.max()
   return v
```
This keeps current sequence; global best unaffected.

Base left inverse sqrt. We want reduce constant plateau. Add a sinusoidal factor with zero mean over [0,n]. The average of factor =1. Normalization may lower p0? For base left, multiplying by exp(γ i/n), γ<0 makes left smaller, sum smaller? normalization increases all, p0 may increase? Let's test: base_i~i^-0.5; multiply exp(-t). ```
def _make_valid(a,n):
  a=np.asarray(a,float)
  a=np.where(np.isfinite(a),a,0); a=np.maximum(a,0)
  if a.sum()<1e-12: a=np.ones(n)
  a = a * (n / a.sum())
  a = np.clip(a,0,1000)
  s=a.sum()
  if s<1e-12: a=np.ones(n)
  else: a = a * (n/s)
  a = np.clip(a,0,1000)
  if a.sum()<0.01: return None
  return a
```
For dense, fine.

import time

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
   start=time.monotonic()
   try: budget=max(1.0,float(budget_s))
   except: budget=1300.0
   deadline=start+max(0.2,budget-2.0)
   if deadline <= start: deadline=start+max(0.1,budget*0.8)
   rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
   # get n
   n=4806; parent=None
   try:
      p=np.asarray(height_sequence_1,dtype=np.float64)
      if p.ndim==1 and p.size>0 and np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
         p=np.clip(p,0,1000)
         if p.sum()>=0.01:
            parent=p; n=int(p.size)
   except Exception: pass
   if n<1: n=4806
   # candidate list
   cands=[]
   if parent is not None: cands.append(parent)
   # if budget small, only inverse sqrt?
   limited = budget < 30
   if not limited:
      i=np.arange(n,dtype=float); t=i/(n if n>1 else 1); i1=i+1.0
      add uniform
      powers...
      mods...
   else:
      i1=np.arange(1,n+1,dtype=float); a=i1**(-0.5); a=_make_valid(a,n); cands.append(a)
   # surrogate score
   scored=[]
   for a in cands:
      av=_make_valid(a,n)
      if av is None: continue
      try:
         cmax=np.max(_conv_full(av))
         score=2*cmax/n
      except: continue
      if np.isfinite(score): scored.append((score,av))
   scored.sort(key=lambda x:x[0])
   best_a=None; best_score=np.inf
   # exact top maybe min(20, ...)
   top_count = 1 if limited else min(25,len(scored))
   for sc,a in scored[:top_count]:
      if time.monotonic()>deadline: break
      eso=_exact_score(a)
      if np.isfinite(eso) and eso<best_score:
         best_score=eso; best_a=a.copy()
   if best_a is None:
      if scored:
         best_a=scored[0][1].copy(); best_score=_exact_score(best_a)
      else:
         best_a=_make_valid(i1**(-0.5),n) if n>0 else np.ones(1)
         best_score=_exact_score(best_a)
   # Polish
   if budget > 60 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 20:
       max_time=min(600.0, max(10.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 20))
       best_a,best_score=_polish(best_a,best_score,deadline,max_time,rng)
   # restarts
   restart_budget = deadline - time.monotonic() - 10
   count=0
   while restart_budget > 60 and count < 4:
       sigma=0.2*(count+1) maybe
       a2=_random_perturb(best_a,rng,sigma)
       if a2 is not None:
           chunk=min(150.0, restart_budget-20)
           best_a,best_score=_polish(a2,best_score,deadline,chunk,rng)
       restart_budget=deadline-time.monotonic()-10
       count+=1
   # final scale and score
   return _finalize(best_a)

```
def _exact_score(a):
   try:
      arr=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
      if arr.size==0: return np.inf
      return float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in arr]))
   except Exception:
      return np.inf
```

Let's see if shape `base*(1 - 0.5*cos(2πt))` (amp0.5) has min at t=0 (0.5 base), max at middle (1.5 base). It reduces left where base high? Base high near left; yes. Normalization? Sum of shape over t with base weight: ∫ t^-0.5(1 -0.5 cos) = base integral -0.5∫ t^-0.5 cos. The integral maybe small. This shifts mass to middle. Likely reduces left plateau but might increase center/right. Could be good.

We include amps up to 0.5 for cos/sin. The exact top might be ~1.5. Good.

Maybe target 1.502 exactly from such a modulated inverse sqrt? Could be.

Now, one more exact evaluation of a candidate in final program after scaling to max1 could produce a slightly different (higher) score than surrogate due to FFT error? Exact is accurate. We choose based on exact top, so okay.

If a mod candidate has coefficients with min very small but positive, exact direct sum okay. Scaling max1 may make min ~1e-5, fine.

Let's finalize code. Need ensure no type hint requiring np import? fine.

```

If we define `_EVAL`, no conflict.

Now, let's write final Python code carefully. Ensure no lines too long? fine.

One subtle thing: `_fft_len` for n large maybe memory; if n>30000, 2n-1~60000 N=65536, still okay. If n>100000, N=262144, memory complex ~4MB each, okay. Direct exact evaluator O(n²) impossible, but not expected. Our time limit maybe exact top heavy. If n large, we should reduce candidate exact count. We'll set `top_count = min(10 if n>10000 else 25, len(scored))`.

Also, in candidate surrogate sort, if n large, FFT still okay but exact direct slow. top_count smaller.

Let's implement `_polish` with `max_time` default. It computes `sqrt_n = np.sqrt(float(n))` once.

In `_polish`, if exact scoring at start takes long, check time after.

The `_polish` exact score inside uses `_exact_score(a)`; if it returns invalid (inf) due to cap? a sum n and max<=1000, sum>0.01, should valid. If invalid, skip.

When perturbing v in `_polish`, recompute a,c,m and set `m` maybe high. We should not update best unless exact improves.

After a perturbation, the line search may not find improvement from the perturbed point; it will reduce step. But it descends from perturbed point. Good.

At end of each outer polish, if `best_local_score` is inf (no valid exact?), return original best.

Let's code `_random_perturb` to ensure `make_valid` valid.

Final scaling: If `best_a` is None, return [1.0]. If exact final invalid, fallback parent? We have best_a valid. Use:
```
aout=np.asarray(best_a).copy()
aout=np.clip(aout,0,1000)
m=np.max(aout)
if m<=0: aout=np.ones(1); m=1
aout = aout/m
if aout.sum()<0.01: aout *= 0.01/aout.sum()
# clip and if sum invalid fallback
try:
  score=_exact_score(aout)
  if not finite: aout=np.ones(1)
except: aout=np.ones(1)
return [float(x) for x in aout]
```
If scaling max1 makes values >1000? no, max1. If sum<0.01 and scale up, max may exceed1000 if very sparse; then maybe fallback. But dense fine.

Let's ensure `_make_valid` for n=4806 returns sum exactly n after two clips. If clipping doesn't change, sum n. If clipping changes, renormalize and clip; max may remain 1000, sum n feasible if number of 1000s enough? For dense no clip.

```python
import numpy as np
import time

try:
    _EVAL = evaluate_sequence
except Exception:
    def _EVAL(sequence):
        if not isinstance(sequence, list):
            return float("inf")
        if not sequence:
            return float("inf")
        for x in sequence:
            if isinstance(x, bool) or not isinstance(x, (int, float)):
                return float("inf")
            if np.isnan(x) or np.isinf(x):
                return float("inf")
        sequence = [float(x) for x in sequence]
        sequence = [max(0.0, x) for x in sequence]
        sequence = [min(1000.0, x) for x in sequence]
        n = len(sequence)
        b = np.convolve(sequence, sequence)
        max_b = max(b)
        sum_a = float(np.sum(sequence))
        if sum_a < 0.01:
            return float("inf")
        return float(2 * n * max_b / (sum_a * sum_a))

_FFT_CACHE = {}

def _fft_len(n):
    n = int(n)
    if n in _FFT_CACHE:
        return _FFT_CACHE[n]
    L = max(1, 2 * n - 1)
    N = 1
    while N < L:
        N <<= 1
    _FFT_CACHE[n] = N
    return N

def _conv_full(a):
    n = int(a.size)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    N = _fft_len(n)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, N)
    return c[: 2 * n - 1].astype(np.float64, copy=False)

def _correl_valid(w, a):
    n = int(a.size)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    N = _fft_len(n)
    W = np.fft.rfft(w, N)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    r = np.fft.irfft(np.conj(W) * A, N)
    return r[:n].astype(np.float64, copy=False)

def _exact_score(a):
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if arr.size == 0:
            return float("inf")
        return float(_EVAL([float(x) for x in arr]))
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")

def _make_valid(a, n):
    try:
        a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
        return None
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return None
    if a.size == 0:
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    elif a.size != n:
        if a.size < n:
            a = np.pad(a, (0, n - a.size), mode="edge")
        else:
            a = a[:n]
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(n)
    a = a * (float(n) / s)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return None
    a = a * (float(n) / s)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if a.sum() < 0.01:
        return None
    return a.astype(np.float64, copy=False)

def _surrogate_score(a):
    n = a.size
    if n <= 0:
        return float("inf")
    cmax = float(np.max(_conv_full(a)))
    if not np.isfinite(cmax):
        return float("inf")
    return 2.0 * cmax / float(n)

def _softmax_weights(c, tau):
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(cmax):
        w = np.zeros_like(c)
        w[np.argmax(c)] = 1.0
        return w
    sh = c - cmax
    ew = np.exp(tau * sh)
    s = float(ew.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        w = np.zeros_like(c)
        w[np.argmax(c)] = 1.0
        return w
    return ew / s

def _from_v(v, n):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(n)
    if v.size != n:
        v = np.pad(v, (0, n - v.size), mode="edge") if v.size < n else v[:n]
    v = np.where(np.isfinite(v), v, -1e6)
    m = float(np.max(v))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        m = 0.0
    v = v - m
    e = np.exp(v)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.full(n, 1.0, dtype=np.float64)
    return (float(n) * e / s).astype(np.float64, copy=False)

def _perturb_v(v, rng, sigma):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    n = v.size
    if n == 0:
        return v
    sigma = float(max(0.01, sigma))
    ar = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for _ in range(3):
        f = float(rng.uniform(0.3, 6.0))
        ph = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        amp = sigma * float(rng.uniform(0.25, 1.0))
        v += amp * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * f * ar / max(1, n) + ph)
    for _ in range(int(rng.integers(1, 4))):
        width = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, min(n, max(2, n // 10)))))
        stop = max(1, n - width + 1)
        start = int(rng.integers(0, stop))
        v[start:start + width] += rng.normal(0.0, sigma * 0.5, size=min(width, n - start))
    v -= float(np.max(v))
    return v

def _random_perturb(a, rng, sigma=0.35):
    n = a.size
    if n <= 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    aa = np.maximum(a, 1e-12)
    s = aa.sum()
    if s <= 0:
        aa = np.ones(n)
        s = n
    aa = aa * (float(n) / s)
    v = np.log(aa)
    v -= float(np.max(v))
    v = _perturb_v(v, rng, sigma)
    out = _from_v(v, n)
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
    os = float(out.sum())
    if os > 0:
        out = out * (float(n) / os)
    return _make_valid(out, n)

def _polish(a0, best_score, deadline, max_time, rng):
    n = int(a0.size)
    if n <= 0:
        return a0, best_score
    best_local_score = float(best_score)
    best_local_a = np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64).copy()

    a = np.maximum(a0, 1e-12).astype(np.float64)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(n)
    a = a * (float(n) / s)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if a.sum() < 0.01:
        return best_local_a, best_local_score

    v = np.log(a)
    v -= float(np.max(v))
    a = _from_v(v, n)
    c = _conv_full(a)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return best_local_a, best_local_score

    sc0 = _exact_score(a)
    if np.isfinite(sc0) and sc0 < best_local_score:
        best_local_score = sc0
        best_local_a = a.copy()

    max_time = max(1.0, float(max_time))
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    step_rms = 0.10
    tau = 5.0
    no_improve = 0
    sqrt_n = float(np.sqrt(n))
    last_exact = 0.0

    for it in range(10000000):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        if time.monotonic() - t0 > max_time:
            break

        if it % 80 == 0 or time.monotonic() - last_exact > 1.2:
            sc = _exact_score(a)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_local_score:
                best_local_score = sc
                best_local_a = a.copy()
            last_exact = time.monotonic()

        w = _softmax_weights(c, tau)
        h = _correl_valid(w, a)
        g = 2.0 * h
        ag = float(np.dot(a, g)) / float(n)
        F = a * (g - ag)
        F = np.where(np.isfinite(F), F, 0.0)
        normF = float(np.linalg.norm(F))

        if not np.isfinite(normF) or normF < 1e-12:
            v = _perturb_v(v, rng, 0.20)
            a = _from_v(v, n)
            c = _conv_full(a)
            m = float(np.max(c))
            if not np.isfinite(m):
                break
            no_improve += 1
            continue

        accepted = False
        best_trial_m = m
        best_trial_v = None
        best_trial_a = None
        best_trial_c = None

        for rms in (step_rms, 0.5 * step_rms, 2.0 * step_rms):
            if time.monotonic() > deadline:
                break
            scale = rms * sqrt_n / normF
            vt = v - scale * F
            if not np.all(np.isfinite(vt)):
                continue
            vt -= float(np.max(vt))
            at = _from_v(vt, n)
            at = np.clip(at, 0.0, 1000.0)
            sas = float(at.sum())
            if sas <= 0:
                continue
            at = at * (float(n) / sas)
            if at.max() > 1000.0:
                continue
            ct = _conv_full(at)
            mt = float(np.max(ct))
            if np.isfinite(mt) and mt < best_trial_m:
                best_trial_m = mt
                best_trial_v = vt
                best_trial_a = at
                best_trial_c = ct

        if best_trial_v is not None:
            v = best_trial_v
            a = best_trial_a
            c = best_trial_c
            m = best_trial_m
            accepted = True
            step_rms = min(0.25, step_rms * 1.07)
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            step_rms = max(0.02, step_rms * 0.82)
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 70:
                v = _perturb_v(v, rng, 0.18)
                a = _from_v(v, n)
                c = _conv_full(a)
                m = float(np.max(c))
                tau = float(rng.uniform(2.0, 22.0))
                step_rms = float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.25))
                no_improve = 0
                if not np.isfinite(m):
                    break

        if it % 200 == 0:
            sc = _exact_score(a)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_local_score:
                best_local_score = sc
                best_local_a = a.copy()
            last_exact = time.monotonic()

        tau = min(40.0, tau + 0.03)
        if tau > 40.0:
            tau = 40.0

    sc = _exact_score(a)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_local_score:
        best_local_score = sc
        best_local_a = a.copy()
    return best_local_a, best_local_score

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    start = time.monotonic()
    try:
        budget = max(1.0, float(budget_s))
    except Exception:
        budget = 1300.0
    deadline = start + max(0.2, budget - 3.0)
    if deadline <= start:
        deadline = start + max(0.1, 0.8 * budget)

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    n = 4806
    parent = None
    try:
        p = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
        if p.ndim == 1 and p.size > 0 and np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
            p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
            if p.sum() >= 0.01:
                parent = p.astype(np.float64, copy=False)
                n = int(p.size)
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    if n < 1:
        n = 4806

    limited = budget < 45.0
    candidates = []

    if parent is not None:
        candidates.append(parent)

    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    nmax = max(1, n)
    t = i / float(nmax)
    i1 = i + 1.0

    if not limited:
        candidates.append(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))

        for beta in (0.0, 0.2, 0.4, 0.5, 0.6, 0.8, 0.95, 1.0):
            candidates.append(i1 ** (-beta))
        for beta in (-0.2, -0.5):
            cand = i1 ** (-beta)
            cand = np.where(np.isfinite(cand), cand, 0.0)
            candidates.append(cand)

        base = i1 ** (-0.5)
        for gamma in (-0.8, -0.4, -0.2, 0.2, 0.4, 0.8, 1.2):
            candidates.append(base * np.exp(gamma * t))

        for amp in (0.15, 0.30, 0.50):
            for sgn in (-1.0, 1.0):
                candidates.append(base * (1.0 + sgn * amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * t)))
                candidates.append(base * (1.0 + sgn * amp * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * t)))
                candidates.append(base * (1.0 + sgn * amp * t))

        mix2 = base * 0.7 + 0.3 * np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        mix3 = base * 0.5 + 0.5 * np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        rev = (n - i)
        mix4 = 0.7 * base + 0.3 * rev ** (-0.5)
        candidates.extend([mix2, mix3, mix4])

        i2 = i + 1.0
        extra = (i2 ** (-0.5)) / (1.0 + 0.25 * t)
        candidates.append(extra)
        candidates.append(extra * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * t / 2.0))
        candidates.append(np.maximum(extra, 1e-9))
    else:
        candidates.append(i1 ** (-0.5))

    scored = []
    for a in candidates:
        av = _make_valid(a, n)
        if av is None:
            continue
        try:
            ss = _surrogate_score(av)
        except Exception:
            ss = float("inf")
        if np.isfinite(ss):
            scored.append((ss, av))

    scored.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])

    best_a = None
    best_score = float("inf")

    top_count = 1 if limited else (12 if n > 8000 else 25)
    for sc, av in scored[:top_count]:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        es = _exact_score(av)
        if np.isfinite(es) and es < best_score:
            best_score = es
            best_a = av.copy()

    if best_a is None:
        if scored:
            best_a = scored[0][1].copy()
            best_score = _exact_score(best_a)
        else:
            tmp = _make_valid(i1 ** (-0.5), n)
            best_a = tmp if tmp is not None else np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
            best_score = _exact_score(best_a)
        if not np.isfinite(best_score):
            best_a = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
            best_score = _exact_score(best_a)

    if budget > 70 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 25:
        chunk = min(700.0, max(10.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 25))
        best_a, best_score = _polish(best_a, best_score, deadline, chunk, rng)

    restart_budget = deadline - time.monotonic() - 10.0
    restarts = 0
    sigmas = [0.25, 0.45, 0.75, 1.1]
    while restart_budget > 70.0 and restarts < len(sigmas):
        a2 = _random_perturb(best_a, rng, sigmas[restarts])
        if a2 is not None:
            chunk = min(180.0, max(20.0, restart_budget - 20.0))
            best_a, best_score = _polish(a2, best_score, deadline, chunk, rng)
        restart_budget = deadline - time.monotonic() - 10.0
        restarts += 1

    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 1.0:
        sc = _exact_score(best_a)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc

    try:
        out = np.asarray(best_a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
        out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
        mx = float(np.max(out))
        if not np.isfinite(mx) or mx <= 0.0:
            out = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
            mx = 1.0
        out = out / mx
        s = float(out.sum())
        if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 0.01:
            out = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            final_score = _exact_score(out)
            if not np.isfinite(final_score):
                out = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
                final_score = _exact_score(out)
        return [float(x) for x in out]
    except Exception:
        return [1.0]
```
